# Parity: Tool surfaces and background features
Baseline: origin/master 6ca499f (2026-11-06). Inventory by lane native-plan-inv-apps.
Rows: 1445   Scope: ui/{screenshot,screenrec,clips,notes,timer,player,project,lookaway,ontop}; lib/features/{screenshot,screenrec,clips,notes,timer,player,project,lookaway,ontop,autopause,apppower,ramreclaim,maxfold,spotify} and their _ helpers, _ocr-text, _tools-hub, _open-timing; lib/apppower-core.js; lib/gif-frame.js; docs/autopause-protocol.md

ID numbers are unique but not always contiguous (SHOT-300+ is the backend, NOTE-900+ the tools hub). Each surface was inventoried from source, and rows say what the code really does, so features that were assumed but do not exist are listed under Not covered rather than invented.

## Screenshot UI

| ID | Area | Function | Electron source | Electron test | Native |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SHOT-001 | Screenshot UI / Overlay session | One overlay page per monitor: main sends `screenshot.frame` {seq, grab, monitor {index,x,y,w,h} physical, windows[] (physical, topmost first), mode, cursor, src (BMP in %TEMP%), pixels?, ocr, problem?}; the page shows THAT monitor's frozen frame filling the viewport exactly | ui/screenshot/overlay.js#onFrame, ui/screenshot/index.html | ui/screenshot/shoot.js, lib/features/screenshot.test.js --electron (#1) | todo |
| SHOT-002 | Screenshot UI / Overlay session | On load the page sends `screenshot.loaded` {at} so main knows the page exists before the first frame | ui/screenshot/overlay.js | none | todo |
| SHOT-003 | Screenshot UI / Overlay session | Ready handshake: the frame is decoded and PAINTED while the window is parked off screen; the page sends `screenshot.ready` {seq, monitor, ms, decodeMs, natural, view, dpr, how, path} after two animation frames, or after a 250 ms timer when the window is hidden (no frames); said once per seq; main shows the window only after it, so the user never sees an empty overlay | ui/screenshot/overlay.js#afterPicture | ui/screenshot/overlay.page.test.js, lib/features/screenshot.test.js --electron (#1 "said ready before the timeout") | todo |
| SHOT-004 | Screenshot UI / Overlay session | Raw-pixels path: when the frame carries `pixels` (RGBA, opaque, top-down) they go straight onto a canvas (putImageData, alpha off) with no image decode; ready.path = "raw pixels"; canvas is the frame's size | ui/screenshot/overlay.js#onFrame,#rawCanvas | ui/screenshot/overlay.page.test.js | todo |
| SHOT-005 | Screenshot UI / Overlay session | Raw pixels that fail (wrong byte count) fall back to decoding the BMP file; ready.path = "BMP decode (raw pixels failed: <reason>)", no problem shown | ui/screenshot/overlay.js#onFrame | ui/screenshot/overlay.page.test.js | todo |
| SHOT-006 | Screenshot UI / Overlay session | The frozen frame (sidecar's 32-bit BI_BITFIELDS BMP with junk in every fourth byte) is shown fully OPAQUE and pixel-exact at the monitor's physical size | ui/screenshot/overlay.js, ui/screenshot/overlay.css#frame | ui/screenshot/shoot.js (check 3, needs MANTLE_SIDECAR_EXE) | todo |
| SHOT-007 | Screenshot UI / Overlay errors | Frame decode fails: centre panel "Screenshot unavailable" / "Mantle could not show the picture of this screen. Press Esc and take the screenshot again." / "Esc closes this"; ready is still sent with problem "the frozen frame of this screen could not be decoded (<ErrName>: <msg>)" for mantle.log, never with a file: or data: address; no jargon on screen | ui/screenshot/overlay.js#onFrame,#fail, lib/plain-error.js | ui/screenshot/overlay.page.test.js | todo |
| SHOT-008 | Screenshot UI / Overlay errors | A frame that arrives with `problem` (main could not grab this screen) shows main's plain words in the problem panel and says ready at once (ms 0, problem); no codes like sidecar/BitBlt/Win32/0x on screen | ui/screenshot/overlay.js#onFrame | ui/screenshot/shoot.js (problem=1) | todo |

| LKA-009 | Look away / Model | Runtime is @mediapipe/tasks-vision 1.1.1 (vision_bundle.js plus wasm/vision_wasm_internal.js and .wasm, from node_modules); task is FaceDetector (not a landmarker) in VIDEO running mode, CPU delegate, minDetectionConfidence 0.5 | ui/lookaway/detector.js#load, ui/lookaway/index.html | lib/features/lookaway.page.test.js | todo |

| LKA-011 | Look away / Model | When the runtime is missing the page fails with "the face model runtime did not load (MediaPipe is missing from node_modules: run npm install)"; a model load error: "the face model could not load: <msg>" | ui/lookaway/detector.js#load, #start | none | todo |

| LKA-014 | Look away / Sampling | A true N-per-second schedule on the camera's own frame rate (a frame is looked at when due within half a frame interval; a stall restarts the schedule instead of bursting); every other frame is closed at once; timestamps always rise | ui/lookaway/detector.js#due, #pump | lib/features/lookaway.page.test.js | todo |
| LKA-015 | Look away / Sampling | Looks per second follow the situation: 0 paused, 1 while the user types or moves the mouse (within 3 s of input), 3 idle watching, 4 while an away streak is timed, 10 while covered (every frame, so looking back reveals at once); main sends `rate` only on a change | lib/features/_lookaway-core.js#rate, lib/features/lookaway.js#act | lib/features/lookaway.test.js, lib/features/lookaway.page.test.js | todo |
| LKA-016 | Look away / Detection | A detection counts as a face only when its six keypoints sit like a face's: eyes apart >= 0.15 of box width, eye-line tilt <= 35 deg, nose 0.15-0.5 and mouth 0.3-0.75 of box height below the eyes (rejects Chromium's fake pattern and round shapes; a full profile is not a face) | ui/lookaway/detector.js#plausible | lib/features/lookaway.page.test.js | todo |
| LKA-017 | Look away / Detection | With several faces in view the largest plausible one is the user (someone behind is smaller) | ui/lookaway/detector.js#observe | lib/features/lookaway.page.test.js | todo |
| LKA-018 | Look away / Pose | Head yaw comes from the nose offset from the eye midpoint against the eye distance, averaged with the ear-distance ratio when both ears are seen (asin(off x 1.4) in degrees); pitch from where the nose sits between the eye line and the mouth ((share - 0.55) x 120); size = face box height / frame height | ui/lookaway/pose.js#fromKeypoints, ui/lookaway/detector.js#observe | lib/features/lookaway.page.test.js | todo |
| LKA-019 | Look away / IPC | Each looked-at frame sends main `lookaway.seen` with numbers only {t, face, score, yaw, pitch, size, ms} (2 decimals; ms 1 decimal); no image or text ever | ui/lookaway/detector.js#pump | lib/features/lookaway.page.test.js | todo |

| MAXF-004 | Maxfold / What | The overlay is click-through, never activated, never delays input; layered + transparent + no-activate + tool window, redirected | sidecar/MaxFold.cs#overlay | sidecar/test-maxfold.js, lib/features/_maxfold-selftest.js | todo |
| MAXF-005 | Maxfold / What | Overlay sits directly above the target window in z-order (Mantle's topmost bar and menus stay above it); topmost band if the target is topmost; at rest hidden and parked at 1x1 | sidecar/MaxFold.cs#ShowOverlay, HideOverlay | sidecar/test-maxfold.js | todo |

## Notes for the port

### Screenshot UI

- No annotation tools exist in this surface: no pen, arrow, text, shapes, crop handles, resize/move of a finished selection, snapping, edit, share or OCR button on the card. A selection is final on mouse-up (Windows' own behaviour). Do not invent them in the port; any such request is new scope.
- Show-after-paint is the core trick: each per-monitor window is parked off screen, gets the frame, paints it, says `screenshot.ready` (two animation frames, or a 250 ms fallback timer when hidden), and only then is moved on screen. The native port must keep "frozen frame already drawn before visible" or the user sees a flash of the live desktop.
- DPI rule: scale = monitor physical size / window client size, never the nominal DPI factor; edges (not origin+size) are rounded so neighbouring selections share edges. One window per monitor with one scale each is what makes mixed DPI correct. Native: use per-monitor-v2 awareness and keep geometry.js's math 1:1 (it is shared with main, so port it once as a pure library and reuse its unit cases).
- The canary (magenta #FF00FF dot, `screenshot.canary`) exists because a capture window that every window flag called "on screen" was invisible (2026-09-24); main reads the pixel back off the screen. A native overlay still needs some proof-of-visibility check before trusting it, plus the fallback to Windows' Snipping Tool with a card that says so.
- Privacy: parked overlay windows and the card drop every picture of the screen (img src, canvas 0x0 / 1x1). Copy text content is shown on the card but never logged. Keep both in native.
- Right button is handled on mousedown, not pointerdown, because a right press chorded during a left drag fires no pointerdown in Chromium; in Win32/WinUI the equivalent is handling WM_RBUTTONDOWN while the left button is captured.
- The card's timer must run in a hidden window (tests rely on it timing out), pauses for hover, KEYBOARD focus (not click focus) and an in-flight Save, and reports each hold to main (`screenshot.cardHold`) because main runs a 25 s backstop after the card's life.
- The card window is sized by main from the card's own measured size (cardShown/cardSize) plus a 12 DIP margin; a native card should own its size directly, but the window must never clip it when text changes ("Path copied", warn lines).
- The card glides with the bar (`screenshot.lift`, shared bar-follow: 336 ms spring up, 300 ms cubic-bezier(.3,0,.8,.15) down, 62 px reach). The spring is a CSS linear() curve with ~41 points; native needs the same curve table (shared with the bar banners).
- Dark theme uses backdrop blur over the page's own frozen frame (overlay: cheap, in-page) and a baked blurred picture of the screen for the card (hud-backdrop, asked once per show and once after a glide). In WinUI the overlay chrome can use an in-app blur of the frame bitmap; the card is a candidate for Acrylic/backdrop instead of the baked picture, but must match the look.
- User-quoted requests: "screenshots shouldnt all get saved to my computer automatically only if i press a button in the popup that says save" (copy by default, Save button; auto-save is an opt-in setting owned by the backend); "the screenshot pop up thing in the bottom right can be not as long now since there only one button now" (content-width card, 240-372 px); dark: "mantle needs to be more see through everything" and "go by what helm looks like".
- Test-harness drift: ui/screenshot/shoot.js expects Tab to walk rect,window,full,text,close, but index.html now has the focusable Record button between Copy text and X, so that check likely fails or is stale (not run here). shoot.js and the page tests skip/abort when a game process is running (VALORANT, javaw, Minecraft, RobloxPlayerBeta). IPC used by this UI: screenshot.frame/ready/loaded/mode/commit/cancel/clear/ocr/record/canary and screenshot.card/cardShown/cardSize/cardLoaded/cardHold/cardSave/cardOpen/cardFolder/cardCopyPath/cardDismiss/cardClear/lift/card.backdrop; sidecar dependencies by name: Screenshot.cs (grab/BMP), Ocr.cs (Copy text).

### Screenshot backend, OCR, GIF frames

- Sidecar dependencies (sidecar/Screenshot.cs): `shot.grab`, `shot.copy`, `shot.save`, `shot.release`, `shot.folder`, `shot.ocr`, `shot.owner`; sidecar/Ocr.cs: `ocr.status`; plus generic `foreground` and `focus` (the AttachThreadInput/Alt-nudge focus ladder, lib/raise.js) and the `keylate` event from the keyboard hook (Hotkey.cs). A native port can call these in-process, but the "frozen frame held by id" contract (grab id, release by id, held while the card is up) must stay.
- The core risk is "proved in front": a capture is only trusted after measured Win32 facts (IsWindowVisible, DWMWA_CLOAKED, GetWindowRect, WindowFromPoint+GA_ROOT, GetForegroundWindow) AND a GetPixel canary. This exists because on 2026-09-24 the overlay logged "on screen" and the user saw nothing while 14 presses were swallowed. Native must keep verify + fallback to `ms-screenclip:`; a WinUI window can lie the same way.
- Foreground is the hard part: the chord is eaten by the low-level hook so the process has no input right; the overlay takes the keyboard while PARKED (off-screen but shown) before moving on screen (KB_FIRST_MS 250). Native needs the same ladder; SetForegroundWindow alone will be refused.
- Parked-not-hidden: hidden windows get no frames, so the frame is painted before the move. In WinUI 3, an off-screen shown window plus DComp may behave differently; measure first-frame latency (Electron target: on screen ~100 ms after the chord, grab 32 ms).
- Per-monitor windows at exact PHYSICAL size are needed for mixed DPI; a DIP-sized window was off by rounding (1536x867 at 125%). Geometry (cssToPhys per-edge rounding) lives in ui/screenshot/geometry.js (UI lane), shared with the saved-rect math here.
- Self-heal timings: 2 fallbacks in a row -> down; retake after 30 s / 2 min / 10 min...; trial = one failure sends back. User-cited outage 2026-09-28 (keys never came back). Shell UI in front gets 1000 ms and is never counted.
- Default since 2026-10-14 is copy-only (user quote in code: "screenshots shouldnt all get saved to my computer automatically only if i press a button in the popup that says save. they should just be copied to clipboard"). Don't regress autoSave default.
- Clipboard is CF_DIB from the sidecar (no PNG): copy p50 was 863 ms when it round-tripped through PNG. Electron 44 has no sync writeImage; native can SetClipboardData directly.
- The card is a shaped (region) window that glides with the bar (lib/bar-follow.js, lib/bar-shape.js, lib/corner.js); the shaped region falling back to "flat" is a real Windows quirk (SetWindowRgn not sticking). Native port must share the bar's spring clock (62 DIP up, 60 ms behind on exit).
- Privacy rules bind the port: no pixel values, titles or paths in logs (noPaths); OCR text never logged; frames under %TEMP%\mantle-shot only and deleted at release, next grab, stop and next start.
- lib/gif-frame.js is NOT used by recordings/clips GIF output (as the task assumed): its only caller is lib/look-desktop.js (main.js gifFrame) for a GIF wallpaper's first-frame still. In C#, WIC's GIF decoder can replace it, but the black-composite and transparent-index rules and the plain-words reasons must match.
- Overlay page UI (selection, Window/Full/Text/Record bar, keyboard selection, size label) and the card page (card.js: hover pause, Ctrl+S, Try again) are in ui/screenshot, inventoried by the UI lane.

### Screen recording and Timer

- Screen recording depends on `Mantle.Sidecar.exe --record` (sidecar/Recorder.cs; `--rec-standin`, `--rec-probe=<file>` for tests) plus sidecar commands `gamemode.scan`, `shot.owner`, `files.clip.set`. Record.cs (`record.target`, `record.keys`, `clips.share`) is the Win+G panel's Game Bar route, not this recorder. The JSON-lines stdin/stdout protocol between Mantle and the recorder is already native C#; a native Mantle can keep the child process or run the recorder in-process. The user's RAM rule ("zero cost idle") says keep it a child process that exits with the file.
- Hidden timings: gamemode.scan 1500 ms, shot.owner 1000 ms, first frame 12 s (main) / 3 s (recorder), stop->done 15 s then kill, rest finish 8 s, recorder checks every 250 ms, tick 1 s, last audio wait <= 400 ms, card life 12 s / 4 s after leave / 5 min backstop, leave fade 200 ms, cross-fade 90 ms.
- WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE: Electron's setContentProtection sometimes left affinity at 0 (e2e 2026-10-16), so the code reads it back and calls SetWindowDisplayAffinity itself. A native port should set 0x11 directly and test it by reading it back.
- The pill window is one fixed 480x248 DIP see-through window that takes clicks only over its shape (setIgnoreMouseEvents forward toggled by hover). In WinUI 3 this needs a layered/transparent window with WM_NCHITTEST or region-based click-through. This is hard; a WebView2 fallback does not help, because the hard part is transparency and click-through.
- The pill-to-card morph is a single element springing its width and height while two faces cross-fade. To match it natively, one Composition visual must be animated, not two windows.
- Win+Shift+S / PrintScreen stopping a running recording lives in screenshot.js's chord handler (via _screenrec-hub). Whoever ports the screenshot overlay must keep "chord while recording = stop". No test covers it today.
- Mouse pointer: never captured in tests. The 2026-10-16 note says a test frame read white because the user's pointer was over the stand-in.
- Timer: main never ticks. One timeout to the wall-clock end plus a monotonic deadline (whichever comes first). powerMonitor resume re-checks. The cell is redrawn only on second boundaries. The native port must keep "no work while idle".
- The timer's state/rules core (_timer-core.js) is shared UMD between main and the top bar page so both use the same arithmetic. Natively, one C# TimerCore should serve both the bar cell and the engine.
- The timer done pop-up never takes focus, so Esc is routed globally by lib/popwatch.js (keyboard hook event `esc`). A native port needs the same global-Esc routing to the newest pop-up.
- The done pop-up always goes on the PRIMARY display. The recording pill follows the target's display. Both are positioned in DIP.
- The top bar's timer cell/menu rows (TIMER-002..027) are drawn by ui/topbar/*. If the top bar lane inventories them too, the parent should de-duplicate them.

### Clips

- There is no Mantle-side capture engine, background buffer, fps, bitrate, resolution or retention logic: recording is Windows' Game Bar (GameDVR), started/stopped by one SendInput of Win+Alt+R; Mantle only watches the Captures folder. Background recording, buffer length, audio, cursor and max length are Windows settings surfaced by sidecar SysGaming.cs (Settings page, out of this lane).
- Hidden timings: poll 1000 ms; start wait 15 s (measured 3.2-7.8 s keys-to-file); end = no write for 4 s; outside-start check 3 s mtime + 700 ms growth; panel idle 90 s; away backstop 5 s; folder watch debounce 400 + 250 ms; hover scrub 120 ms; toast 4.5/8 s; delete wait 15 s; encode silence limit 30 min.
- Hard natively: a WinUI window that never activates (WS_EX_NOACTIVATE), sits at screen-saver topmost, is excluded from capture (SetWindowDisplayAffinity WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE), and is SetWindowRgn-shaped to the card with a read-back verify; all doable in Win32, but must be proved on the real desktop.
- The lossless MP4 cutter (_clips-mp4.js) is pure Node byte work; port it to C# 1:1 with the same fixtures (clips-mp4.test.js builds synthetic movies) or keep it in the sidecar. Windows' MediaTranscoder cannot do lossless cuts.
- Gallery inline play and hover scrub rely on Chromium's <video> (fastSeek, .webm/.mkv). WinUI MediaPlayerElement handles mp4/H.264 but .webm/.mkv need extensions; a WebView2 fallback for the viewer is a candidate.
- Anti-cheat rule (Vanguard): never inject keys into a game; enforced twice (core recordPlan and sidecar record.keys). Keep both layers.
- Incident rule: a test once recorded ~8 s of a real window; tests must never send a real Win+Alt+R (fake recorder, dry runs, passive sidecar stand-in check).
- User quotes cited in code: Win+G is used "mostly just for recording"; "it should open a mantle"; of the corner, "just have them smoothly move up if the taskbar comes up"; no overlapping UI anywhere.
- Sidecar dependencies by name: record.target, record.keys, clips.share (Record.cs); gaming.get, gaming.set (SysGaming.cs); clips.reencode, clips.encoding, clips.encoded, clips.synth (ClipsEncode.cs); files.thumbs, files.clip.set, files.op, files.opdone, files.recycle.restore; the `windows` event.
- Shared pieces the panel depends on: lib/bar-follow.js + ui/shared/bar-follow.js (lift), lib/bar-shape.js (region), lib/corner.js (banners), lib/popwatch.js (outside/Esc/away), lib/raise.js, ui/shared/hud-backdrop.js (dark), _wall-window.js + wall-bdrop.js (gallery dark).
- Pages talk to main through mantle.call names prefixed `clips.` (state, record, mic, size, close, open, list, thumbs, trim, copy, share, delete, undo, folder, panel, painted, win) and pushes clips.state, clips.hide, clips.lift, clips.changed, clips.focus, clips.encoding.

### Notes, Always on top, Tools hub

- Notes text is PULLED, never pushed: the page only sends `notes.dirty {rev}` and main reads the text with executeJavaScript (`window.__notesTake`, 1500 ms timeout) because main's call log writes arguments of failed calls to mantle.log. A native port must keep note text out of every logged channel; with no IPC log in-process this is easier but the "counts only, no paths in log" rule (path regex scrubber) must survive.
- Notes timings: debounce 400 ms, max wait 3 s, "Saving…" after 0.7 s, retry every 5 s while unsaved, undo 8 s, double-delete gap 400 ms, blur grace 400 ms, focus hand-over check 80 ms, window destroyed after 10 min closed (kept while unsaved), find re-run 120 ms.
- Notes store safety is the hardest port item: atomic tmp+rename with O_EXCL and 10x100 ms retry, re-stat before every rename (OneDrive landing mid-save), outside-change copy with a 10-per-10-min cap, 8 MB / 500 notes / UTF-8 refusals, symlink and hard-link checks on deleted.md. lib/features/notes.test.js holds ~100 checks that should be ported as the native store's test list.
- The live notes path uses literally %USERPROFILE%\Documents (not the Documents known folder, which can be redirected to OneDrive). Do not switch to SHGetKnownFolderPath(FOLDERID_Documents).
- The find mark is a mirror div painted behind a textarea because Chromium paints no selection in an unfocused textarea. A WinUI TextBox/RichEditBox has the same problem (selection hidden without focus), so the native version needs its own highlight overlay (or RichEditBox with a highlight range) that does not move focus out of the find box.
- Pad focus: Electron focus() loses to the foreground lock, so the sidecar `focus` command hands focus over after 80 ms, and closing gives focus back to the hwnd from `foreground` (or the one the top bar sent). Native must do the same (AllowSetForegroundWindow / AttachThreadInput tricks) and keep the "click-away closes, no refocus" vs "Esc closes, refocus" split.
- Both pop-ups are raised on EVERY show via lib/raise.js (notes at the pop-up-menu band, keep-on-top pop-up at the screen-saver band) because a just-pinned TOPMOST window would otherwise cover the toast. Native: re-assert HWND_TOPMOST after each show.
- Keep on top depends on sidecar/WindowTop.cs commands `ontop.front`, `ontop.info`, `ontop.check`, `ontop.set` (set on the slow lane), and on sidecar `foreground` / `focus` for notes; the chords Win+Alt+N and Win+Ctrl+T must be in the hook's chord list (sidecar/AltTab.cs ChordNames). Morph.cs is not used by either surface.
- Ownership rule: Mantle only removes TOPMOST it added (`was:false, changed:true`), pins carry the pid, and a crash is recovered by adopting saved pins still on top (lane windows-layouts, 2026-09-29: before that a pinned video dropped behind with no word). The native port must keep the `pins` setting key and shape so a switch from Electron to native adopts existing pins.
- Game rest: notes keeps unsaved text across the rest in module scope; keep on top takes every pin off top for the game and restores it after. In the native single process "module scope" is just a service that outlives the feature restart.
- Dark theme for both pop-ups follows the user's request "mantle needs to be more see through everything" (lane see-huds, 2026-10-09): one fill layer plus a blurred screen backdrop (`notes.backdrop` / `ontop.backdrop` via ui/shared/hud-backdrop.js). Natively that is a candidate for Acrylic/Mica-alt backdrop on the pop-up window.
- The open-timing line format is parsed by tests (settings.test.js, taskmgr.test.js regexes); the native Settings / Task Manager should log the same line so the open-budget checks carry over.

### Media player

- Every playback, decode, subtitle-render, audio-track and fullscreen behaviour rides on Chromium's `<video>` stack: canPlayType MIME probes decide the verdict (PLAY-031..038), MediaError codes 2/3/4 drive the wording (PLAY-041), `audioTracks` needs the blink feature AudioVideoTracks (PLAY-071), `<track>` WebVTT shows subtitles, and navigator.mediaSession feeds SMTC. A native port must re-map all of this to MediaPlayerElement / Windows.Media.Playback (MediaPlaybackItem.AudioTracks, TimedTextSources, SystemMediaTransportControls). Codec support changes: WinRT decodes AVI/WMV/WMA/MPEG-2/AC-3 natively, Chromium does not. So the verdict table and the "never claim AVI/WMV" assoc rule must be re-derived, not copied. If exact parity matters more, a WebView2 host for the stage is the fallback; flag it for the coordinator.
- No ffprobe: probing is pure byte sniffing (_player-probe.js, shared by main and the page as a UMD script). It can be ported straight to C#; the unit tests in lib/features/player.test.js build synthetic boxes and are a ready test spec.
- Sidecar commands by name: taskmgr.frame (window caption tint), files.thumbs (shell thumbnails, px 320, mode 'thumb'), files.openwithdialog (Windows Open with dialog owned by the HWND). Natively these become direct IShellItemImageFactory / SHOpenWithDialog / DwmSetWindowAttribute calls.
- Timings: resume flush 15 s (immediate on pause/close), page report every 5 s, queue save debounce 400 ms, bounds save debounce 700 ms, library stale 10 min, search debounce 150 ms, full-screen idle 2.5 s, toast 4.5 s / 7 s with action, thumbnail batch delay 60 ms, Previous restart threshold 3 s, resume window 10 s from start to 15 s before the end.
- Race fix: saveResume uses the PREPARED item's path, never the queue cursor, because during a switch the index already names the next file while the element still plays the old one (lane mantle-player2). A generation counter makes the newest pick win. Keep both.
- Launcher race fix: the stand-in row keeps the Store app's AUMID, but launching it must open Mantle's window, never the Store app (the e2e caught it running Media Player). The ranking must put Mantle Player first even after Windows' Store list loads.
- Single-instance hand-off: Windows runs `--open=player --play=%1` per file; a second process forwards to the running Mantle. If none is running it shows a blocking error box (pre-ready) and exits 3. A native single-instance (AppInstance redirection) needs the same no-silent-failure box.
- Mini player uses always-on-top level 'floating' and the work area of the display matching the current bounds (multi-monitor aware). Restoring uses the bounds saved at mini time.
- Default-apps registration is a HKCU-only, reversible write with a recorded undo log (settings key `assoc`); the command line embeds process.execPath + app path, which changes for a native exe. The e2e proves zero reg.exe calls in --test via a counter.
- The user's request that started it (2026-10-17): "also we need to replace media player with a custom manlte version". No-silent-failure rule (2026-09-12): an unplayable file names its container and codec in plain words plus a way out.
- The video stage is #0d0e10 in both themes, and controls over a picture use fixed white alpha. Everything else uses the island tokens.

### Project / cast and Look away

- Look-away is a privacy blur, not a break reminder: there is NO break-reminder schedule, snooze, notification, or "rest your eyes" timer anywhere in lookaway.js, the core or the page. Its only output is asking the unfold cover (lib/features/unfold.js via _unfold-bus.js, another lane) to blur/unblur.
- The look-away detector is MediaPipe FaceDetector (BlazeFace short range, 6 keypoints), CPU delegate, VIDEO mode, NOT a face/pose landmarker and no gaze; "yaw/pitch" are keypoint proxies (pose.js). A native port can run the same .tflite via the TFLite C API / ONNX-converted BlazeFace, or keep a hidden WebView2 page; but WebView2 needs MediaStreamTrackProcessor (Chromium 94+, present) and the in-memory session/permission/request-cancel hardening re-done via CoreWebView2 PermissionRequested + WebResourceRequested. Windows.Media.Capture + MediaFrameReader at 320x240 is the natural native frame source; the plausibility LIMITS and pose formulas must be copied exactly (they were tuned by measurement).
- Thresholds to keep bit-exact: delay 1.5 s (0.5-10), quiet 3 s, warm-up 1.2 s, stale 1.6 s, min size 0.5, keep 40, minKeep 4, relearn 5 of 8, rates 0/1/3/4/10, retry 10/30/120 s, park 20 s, governor 1 s with full-screen every 2 and camera-use every 3 ticks.
- Other-app-on-camera uses the HKCU CapabilityAccessManager ConsentStore webcam registry (read via koffi/advapi32); the native port must exclude its OWN exe (key name = exe path with \ as #, lower-cased), which changes from electron.exe to the new exe.
- Full-screen pause depends on the sidecar `foreground` verb (result.fullscreen boolean); sleep/lock via Electron powerMonitor (native: WTS session notifications + PBT_APMSUSPEND); input time via powerMonitor.getSystemIdleTime (native: GetLastInputInfo).
- Look-away code comments name the user; the native port should keep the behaviour but write "the user".
- Project depends on sidecar verbs `sysdisplay.project`, `sysdisplay.topology` (mode, keepWithinMs, dryRun), `sysdisplay.keep`, `sysdisplay.revert`, the `sysdisplay` event (what reverted/kept, token, ok, reason), and `keys` (combo win+k); SetDisplayConfig SDC_TOPOLOGY_* is in sidecar/SysDisplay.cs. The 15 s revert lives in the sidecar so a crash still reverts: keep that property natively (a WinUI process dying must not strand a black screen).
- Project is deliberately cold (window made per key press): a native version may keep a lightweight warm window but must still meet <= 400 ms p95 and draw from the cached read before the fresh one.
- Cast has no own picker: the user's request "i want you to do all of them" was answered by handing Cast to Windows' Win+K panel because no public API starts a whole-screen Miracast session. A native port could revisit Windows.Media.Miracast / ProjectionManager, but parity is "presses Win+K".
- Project pop-up focus after show needs the raise.keyboard ladder (foreground-lock workaround via the sidecar); natively, the hook-delivered Win+P gives foreground rights only if handled carefully (AllowSetForegroundWindow / SendInput trick).
- ui/project/mock-mantle.js is a test/picture harness only (?displays=1|2, ?current=, ?ask=1, ?dark=1); not a user feature.
- The keep-question hairline drain uses a single CSS transition from the remaining fraction to 0 over the remaining ms; the count ticks from main every 1 s.

### Auto-pause

- No headphone-unplug, lock-screen, sleep, call or device-change trigger exists; the only inputs are SMTC media sessions (main's NOW PLAYING read) and per-app audio peaks. Only Spotify is ever controlled, and only through its own SMTC session (GlobalSystemMediaTransportControlsSessionManager), never media keys.
- `_autopause-core.js`, `_autopause-compat.js`, `_autopause-link.js` and `docs/autopause-protocol.md` are byte-identical with Quartz; a native port must keep the HTTP protocol v2 wire-exact (routes, status fields, `sig` format `spotifyState|wePaused|holds|repauseSuppressed`, src regex) or Quartz and the old browser extension break.
- All rule constants are measured, not guessed: pollMs 250, liveness 3000, resumeDelay 4000 (below the smallest inter-blip gap 6708), peak 0.0008 (quietest lesson beat 0.000846), posOnly 8000, minHold 6000, repause 2 then 300000 cooldown, repauseCheck 1000, resumeGrace 1500, httpTtl 180000, anyMinRun 2000. Port them as-is.
- Link timings: probe 600 ms, call 4000 ms, watch wait 250000 (host answers by 240000), refresh 60000, legacy poll 3000, none poll 10000, re-elect 150, handoff gap 2000, anti-hot-loop 40 ms. Coexist 15000, start quiet 5000, warming max 8 asks.
- Natively the meter (sidecar AutoPause.cs) can move in-process, but keep it on its own BelowNormal MTA thread that sleeps when not asked for 2.5 s; the first cut on a request lane caused other commands ("foreground", "place", "gamemode.scan") to time out.
- The process-chain walk (up to 8 ancestors, package family per pid) is essential: Store lesson apps play from a grandchild msedgewebview2.exe; metering the app's own pid reads 0 forever.
- Adoption was a user-reported fix (restart mid-video paused the music; the user pressed play twice and was fought twice): never pause what already plays when auto-pause takes charge on its own; a manual turn-on cancels it.
- Coexistence reads the SpotifyAutoPause task XML directly (readable without elevation) and falls back to schtasks.exe; never PowerShell. An exception means "stay off".
- The Spotify bar card (ui/bar/spotify.js, out of scope here) consumes `autopause.status` (line under the head, state under More) and calls `autopause.switch`; inventory it with the bar widgets.
- Sidecar dependencies by name: `autopause.peaks` (AutoPause.cs), `media` op control with dryRun (Media.cs); host hooks `host.media()`, `host.onMedia()`, `host.features.setEnabled()`.
- Spotify-state race: after a live command, the expected state is assumed for 2 s; without this the next 250 ms tick reads the stale session and double-acts.

### App power, RAM reclaim, Maxfold

- App power has no Node-side timers: the sidecar watcher (2 s, BelowNormal thread) does all re-application; command timeouts 5 s (rules/gpupref), 6 s (caps), 8 s (trim). A native port must keep the job-object design (named `Local\Mantle.AppPower.<FNV-1a(key)>.<rootPid>`, SYNCHRONIZE handle duplicated into the app, orphan sweep via NtQueryDirectoryObject) or limits become unliftable after a restart.
- App power's PROTECTED list (Node) and Never list (sidecar) differ (Node also has searchhost, dllhost, conhost, spoolsv...); the game list is GameMode.GamePrefixes only (NOT the wider OtherNeverPrefixes), shared with Task Manager. Keep ONE list per purpose; taskmgr.test.js checks the game list against _gamemode-plan.js.
- App power state is read per TOP PROCESSES row of the top bar's CPU/RAM/GPU dropdowns via `live.decorate` inside lib/features/topbar.js onStats; the native top bar must merge this state into each stats sample.
- "Free up memory" (EmptyWorkingSet) has no cooldown and no automatic schedule anywhere; there is no automatic RAM trimming in either feature.
- RAM reclaim is registry switches through reg.exe (not PowerShell, not a native module) with a 15 s timeout + one retry: the comment cites a 4 s reg.exe query stopped at Mantle's start (2026-10-15). Natively, use the registry API but keep the journal-first rule, the read-back and the allowlist.
- RAM reclaim's journal is shared with tools/ramreclaim-undo.ps1 (PowerShell 5.1, runs without Mantle); the file path, schema (`v:1`, entries) and allowlist must stay byte-compatible or crash recovery breaks.
- The sign-out rule depends on the stop reason text "Windows is ending the session" from the feature host; a native host must pass an equivalent session-ending signal (WM_QUERYENDSESSION / WM_ENDSESSION).
- User quote (RAM reclaim header, 2026-10-15): "everything should be taken over. also if there is anything we can disable from windows and take over with mantle lets do that. preferably using less ram". App power: "i want to be able to do the cpu limits and gpu and ram and stuff from here".
- Maxfold is already native C# in the sidecar (DWM thumbnails, WinEvent hook, layered overlay); the port is mostly moving MaxFold.cs into the native process plus the Node token reader. Its thread must stay separate from the UI thread (frame loop timing, first frame <= 8 ms).
- Maxfold's only Windows change is DWMWA_TRANSITIONS_FORCEDISABLED on one foreground maximized window; it cannot be read back, hence the record file `maxfold-windows.json` and the restore roads (next start, first config, sidecar exit, restore-windows.ps1 f5).
- Maxfold reads motion tokens from ui/shared/unfold.css and glass-light.css at install; a native port with no CSS needs these numbers (240 ms, Duo curve, blurFrac .0077 from 6/28 px) in its own token source.
- Sidecar commands by name: apppower.rules / status / trim / read / caps / gpupref; taskmgr.snapshot (RAM reclaim memory); maxfold.config / state / decide / flag / pose / stall. Events: `apppower`, `maxfold`.

### Spotify helpers

- The whole "Up next" bridge is plain HTTP + WebSocket CDP to 127.0.0.1:47292; no WebView2 is needed natively. The PAGE function (lib/features/_spotify-app.js lines 128-413) must ship VERBATIM as a JS string: it walks Spotify's React fiber tree and the registry (`Symbol.for("PlayerAPI")`, LibraryAPI, FocusMainWindowAPI, LifecycleAPI, getGraphQLLoader). Port it as a resource file, not a rewrite. The built-in fallback GraphQL hashes are for Spotify 1.3.0.277.
- Exact launch signature is a contract: the takeover after a Mantle restart only accepts a root Spotify.exe whose command line has `--remote-debugging-port=47292 --minimized --disable-gpu` as exact tokens. A native launcher must pass the same three args, or Spotify processes started by the Electron Mantle (and the other way round) will not be taken over.
- Restart deliberately starts WITHOUT `--disable-gpu` and NOT hidden (the user opened it), so it is never taken over as a hidden one. Keep this asymmetry.
- Sidecar commands used: `spotify.mem`, `spotify.trim` (sidecar/SpotifyMem.cs), `audio {op:'sessions'}` (active sessions on the default output), `gamemode.scan {procs:true}`. Natively these can be direct Win32 calls, but the SpotifyMem guard (image path = own %APPDATA%\Spotify\Spotify.exe, parent chain to the root, start time pinned, recheck on the trimming handle) must be kept exactly.
- Electron also shells out: tasklist.exe (running check, fallback game scan), taskkill.exe /PID /T [/F], and PowerShell for the CIM process list (pid, ppid, exe, cmd line, created), the visible-window check (`MainWindowHandle != 0`) and info()'s memory snapshot (a perf-counter read). Native should replace all of these with Toolhelp/NtQuery/EnumWindows; note `tasklist /V` is wrong for visibility (it shows the hidden "OleMainThreadWndName" window of a tray-only Spotify).
- Trim line 400 MB was chosen from measurements: while music plays the renderer refills to ~355-366 MB within 30 s of a trim, so 300 MB only bought 30 s per trim. The user asked for "when spotify creeps up to a specific ram usage instead of doing time".
- The root Spotify process is NEVER trimmed (it owns the audio, and a session on a non-default output is not seen by the audio read). If which process plays cannot be read, nothing is trimmed that tick.
- Game close quote: "for the spotify thing i closed it myself to save ram" (~300 MB in Valorant). The game close is never forced; only the card's Quit/Restart may use `/F`, and only on one pid tree, never by image name.
- Revive exists because the hidden Spotify died in 3 of 3 Valorant sessions with no Windows crash record; the held handle in SpotifyMem.cs is the only way the exit code is known. Native needs an equivalent held SYNCHRONIZE handle and the "zombie still in the process list" check.
- Hide retry exists because at 100% CPU the first hide timed out at 6 s and was never retried; the retry ladder (2/5/15/30 s then 60 s, max 12, growing waits 10-30 s) and the "look first, a late hide counts" rule must be kept.
- Card-facing words are tested to be jargon-free (spotify-holes.test.js JARGON regex); all technical detail goes to the log, deduplicated and scrubbed of Spotify addresses and Windows paths.
- The user accepted the costs knowingly (the source says so): Spotify must be started by Mantle, any local program can use the port while open, it is against Spotify's terms, and a Spotify update can break it. The Web API path was removed because it now needs Premium.

## Not covered

### Screenshot UI

- Theme switching itself (how `data-theme` reaches these pages) lives in shared code outside ui/screenshot; only its effect on these pages is listed.
- Backend behaviour (chords Win+Shift+S / PrintScreen, save folder, auto-save setting, clipboard, OCR, hard timeouts, fallback/self-healing, recording) is the backend lane's (lib/features/screenshot.js); referenced here by IPC name only.

### Screenshot backend, OCR, GIF frames

- ui/screenshot/* (overlay and card pages, geometry.js beyond the helpers cited) by design: another lane.
- lib/features/_screenrec-hub.js / screenrec.js internals (only the handoff is listed), lib/bar-follow.js, lib/bar-shape.js, lib/corner.js and lib/raise.js internals (only how the screenshot feature uses them).
- The idle-unload timeout value for the card (IDLE.IDLE_UNLOAD_MS in lib/features/_idle-unload.js) was not read.
- How the feature loader handles chords during game rest (rest mechanism is out of scope); SHOT-413 marks the feature's own reaction as live-only.

### Screen recording and Timer

- GIF output: there is none. Recording is MP4 only (H.264 + AAC); no GIF code exists in the screenrec files.
- Screen recording has no hotkeys of its own beyond Win+Shift+S/PrintScreen (stop) and V (overlay toggle). There is no pause/resume for a recording, and no region-move while recording.
- The timer has no alarm (time-of-day) or pomodoro mode. The modes are countdown timer, stopwatch and snooze. The Quick settings "Focus" tile is a 30 min timer plus Do not disturb (TIMER-080).
- I only checked from the code how Mantle Settings renders the screenrec options (generic `options` rendering in lib/features.js / Settings). I did not inventory the Settings page.

### Clips

- Features named in the task brief that do not exist in this code, so there are no rows: an instant-replay buffer of Mantle's own, a "save last N seconds" hotkey, rename, GIF export, MP4 export (other than "Save trimmed copy"), "reveal" that selects the file (Show in folder only opens the folder), game auto-start of recording, storage quota or retention.
- ClipHistory.cs (clipboard history, Win+V) and EdgeClip.cs (keeps Explorer's taskbar down) are not part of Clips despite their names; left to their own lanes.
- The Game Bar capture settings (background recording, buffer length, audio, mic default, cursor, policy lock) in sidecar SysGaming.cs belong to the Settings > Gaming surface, not inventoried here.
- Mantle's own region/window recorder (screenrec) is only covered as the panel's entry row.

### Notes, Always on top, Tools hub

- Notes features the task listed that do not exist in the Electron code (nothing to port): per-note pin, colour, window position/size state, import/export, rich formatting, lists. The pad's size and place are fixed (see NOTE-017).
- The top bar's notes icon itself (lib/features/topbar.js, ui/topbar) is only referenced as a trigger; its own rows belong to the top bar lane.
- The shared HUD backdrop (ui/shared/hud-backdrop.js, glass-adapt.js) and lib/raise.js are only referenced; their behaviour belongs to the shared-infrastructure lane.

### Media player

- How the Settings > Mantle feature list renders the two player options (generic feature-option UI, outside this lane's scope); only the keys, labels and defaults are listed.
- Exact pixel layout of the transport/rail in player.css beyond the mode states (full, mini, idle, inactive, 760 px breakpoint).

### Project / cast and Look away

- Exact sizes of the MediaPipe wasm files (vision_wasm_internal.js / .wasm): node_modules is not present in the read-only worktree; version from package.json is 1.1.1.
- The Project and Cast tiles' own rendering in quick settings (ui/bar/qs-tiles.js) belongs to the bar-widgets scope; only their state/wording from _project-core.js#tile and the commands they call are listed here.
- The unfold cover itself (blur drawing, reveal-on-input) belongs to the unfold lane.

(none)

It belongs to the top bar lane.
